1. Key findings · Written by the report model

  • The file is an unsigned x64 DLL named as an EXE, with four exports (extra, follower, run, scub), five imports and a linker of 14.0, and no recognised packer signature. [ev_0001, ev_0003, ev_0004]
  • VirusTotal flags the file as malicious in 56 of 75 engines, but the labels disagree on family, naming Latrodectus most often and IcedID in several cases, so the reputation supports maliciousness rather than a specific family. [ev_0018, ev_0014]
  • The local YARA corpus returns no hits, so the verdict rests on code analysis and reputation, not on local signatures. [ev_0007]
  • Its strings are XOR-encoded in .data and decoded at run time by routine 0xae78, which yields 81 recovered strings and 101 decoded blobs; the plain-text strings contain no indicators. [ev_0019, ev_0021, ev_0005, ev_0006]
  • It resolves about 164 Windows API and module names at run time from CRC-32 hash values, so its imports do not describe its capability; the CRC-32 constant and the capa-reported routines are consistent with this, though individual hash values were not reproduced. [ev_0020, ev_0008, ev_0023]
  • It carries two decoded candidate C2 URLs, hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ and hxxps://skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/, and a WinINet beacon that uses a POST verb, a form-urlencoded report and a user-agent string; the code and the strings are present, but the sandbox recorded no HTTP session to either domain. [ev_0019, ev_0021, ev_0020, ev_0013, ev_0026]
  • The beacon report is RC4-encrypted under the decoded text key "12345" and base64-encoded before posting, as the code is read; no RC4 constants are present, so this depends on the code reading. [ev_0019, ev_0021, ev_0008, ev_0023]
  • Decoded command strings route host discovery through a cmd.exe pipe routine, covering systeminfo, ipconfig, whoami, domain trust and group queries and a WMI antivirus query; the commands were not observed to run. [ev_0019, ev_0021, ev_0025, ev_0012]
  • Code paths fetch a body over WinINet and either write it through NtCreateFile and NtWriteFile and start it with CreateProcessW or rundll32, or run it from a VirtualAlloc buffer on a new thread; no dropped file was recorded, so the download-and-run path is a code-level capability only. [ev_0025, ev_0021, ev_0015, ev_0026]
  • Code registers a logon-triggered scheduled task named Updater through the Task Scheduler COM interface, which we assess is the main persistence mechanism; the registration itself was not observed because the sandbox report has no services or tasks section, and no Run-key write appears in the reached code. [ev_0021, ev_0025, ev_0026]
  • The sandbox recorded three processes: two rundll32 launches of the sample with the #1 export, which is the analysis harness's invocation, and an unattributed svchost.exe -k GPSvcGroup process whose link to the sample is not established. [ev_0012, ev_0028]
  • The 576-second packet capture holds 14 external conversations, none of which is attributed to the sample; no DNS or HTTP to either candidate C2 domain appears, and no regular beacon interval is detected. [ev_0017, ev_0013, ev_0028]
  • The sandbox signatures name a Latrodectus loader, but the sandbox reported no API-call trace, no registry activity, no tasks and no dropped files, so the sandbox run does not confirm the code-level behaviour. [ev_0014, ev_0026, ev_0015]
  • Open questions remain: whether the scheduled task was registered, whether the C2 replies ever supply command codes, whether any fetch returned a body and ran it, and the family; the mutex name is the decoded 'runnung' that the code passes to CreateMutexW [ev_0021][ev_0025]. [ev_0026, ev_0028, ev_0018]

The sample is a 64-bit Windows DLL classified as malware with High severity. We assess with moderate confidence that it is a Latrodectus-family downloader and backdoor (attribution confidence 0.70), though engine labels disagree and the family is not confirmed [ev_0014, ev_0018]. Its main risk is code-level capability to fetch files over WinINet, write them to a per-user folder and run them, alongside an HTTP beacon to two configured URLs. No C2 contact, download or task registration was observed in the sandbox run or the packet capture [ev_0013, ev_0015, ev_0017]. Affected hosts should be isolated, any host that loaded or executed this DLL should be investigated, and the two candidate C2 domains should be blocked at the perimeter until a monitored run settles the open questions.

3. Verdict and assessment · Assessed by the judge

Verdict: Malware (high confidence, 0.92, stated by the judge)

Severity: [HIGH]

Why, in the judge's words:

The sample is a 60,928-byte x64 DLL that 56 of 75 engines flag (ev_0018). Its code contains a complete implant chain: decoded C2 URL candidates and an RC4-encrypted HTTP beacon with numeric command dispatch (ev_0021, ev_0067, ev_0082, ev_0090); a download-and-execute path that writes a fetched body through NtCreateFile and NtWriteFile and runs it through CreateProcessW or from memory (ev_0067, ev_0068, ev_0081, ev_0069); a logon-triggered scheduled task named Updater built through Task Scheduler COM (ev_0077, ev_0092); self-deletion by alternate-stream rename and delete-on-close (ev_0069, ev_0021); and command discovery routed through cmd.exe (ev_0067, ev_0021). Nothing was observed at run time: the sandbox shows no C2 contact, no dropped file, no child cmd.exe and no task telemetry (ev_0013, ev_0026, ev_0112). Gates in 0x3868 may stall the export before the beacon starts, so the rating is High rather than Critical. The family rests on the sandbox signature and the extracted configuration, not on static features, and the sources disagree on whether IcedID overlaps.

Category: downloader / backdoor (Latrodectus-family implant) (a behavioural class, not a family)

Family: Latrodectus (moderate-to-high confidence, 0.70, stated by the judge) [ev_0014, ev_0106]

Analyst agreement: the analysts reached consensus after 3 round(s); 1 listed line was marked not blocking by the mediator (Measured).

4. Execution flow · Written by the report model

Each step is marked as the report model marked it: observed in the sandbox, or assessed from the code and the analysts' claims.

  1. The sandbox launched the file as a DLL through rundll32.exe with the #1 export ordinal, in two rundll32 processes; this is the harness launch, not code the sample builds. A third process, svchost.exe -k GPSvcGroup, was observed and is not attributed to the sample. (observed in sandbox) [ev_0012]
  2. Entry: the four exports extra, follower, run and scub all resolve to RVA 0x3ce4. Export extra calls 0x3cb4, which calls the main routine 0x3868. (assessed) [ev_0004, ev_0025, ev_0081, ev_0077]
  3. Routine 0x3868 resolves its Windows APIs by CRC32 hash through the tables at 0x84e4, 0x904c, 0x9484, 0xa59c and 0xac50, and runs environment gates before the beacon starts: a PEB BeingDebugged read (0x8690, 0x309c, 0x3074), an OS-version check (0x8560), a process-count check (0x8820) and an adapter check (0x68e8). A failed gate returns -1, and 0x3cb4 then loops on Sleep(1000) without returning. (assessed) [ev_0020, ev_0024, ev_0025, ev_0008, ev_0077]
  4. 0x3868 creates a named mutex through slot 0x10c60 and fails when GetLastError returns 0xb7 (ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS). The mutex name is the decoded string 'runnung', which the code passes to CreateMutexW as argument 3 on the path where every conditional jump falls through [ev_0021][ev_0025]. (assessed) [ev_0019]
  5. On the success path, 0x3868 calls 0x6988, which decodes the two C2 URLs hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ and hxxps://skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/ into a two-entry table, and calls 0xcf14, which reads the RC4-encrypted configuration file named update_data.dat. (assessed) [ev_0021, ev_0019, ev_0025]
  6. Install branch: 0x3868 uses the result of 0x378c, which tests whether the install copy exists (NtCreateFile through 0x77b0). When the copy is absent, 0x33ac copies the running image to %APPDATA%\Custom_update\Update_<hex>.<ext>, with the name derived from a volume value through 0x30b0 and 0x3194 and the extension chosen by the image-path check in 0x463c. (assessed) [ev_0021, ev_0025, ev_0019]
  7. 0x33ac calls 0x3728, then 0xb344, which creates the Task Scheduler object (CoInitializeEx and CoCreateInstance in 0xc5e8) and calls 0xc42c. 0xc42c adds a logon trigger with Id 'LogonTrigger' (0xc2c4), an exec action (0xc19c), principal and settings (0xc8c4, 0xc7b4), then calls RegisterTaskDefinition through vtable slot +0x88 with flags 6 under the name 'Updater'. Registration is not observed. (assessed) [ev_0021, ev_0025, ev_0019, ev_0022]
  8. After the copy and task steps, 0x33ac calls 0x3a24, which opens the running image with DELETE access, renames its default data stream to ':wtfbbq' through SetFileInformationByHandle class 3, and sets delete-on-close (class 4). It then starts the copy through CreateProcessW (0xb400, rundll32.exe in DLL mode) and calls ExitProcess. (assessed) [ev_0021, ev_0025, ev_0019]
  9. 0x6250 starts the beacon thread 0x5750 with CreateThread. The beacon waits about 180 s (1800 × 100 ms) and then sleeps 450 to 599 s between cycles, or 1500 to 2099 s when a flag is set. (assessed) [ev_0025, ev_0021, ev_0019]
  10. On its first cycle the beacon gathers host data through 0x7fa8 (GetComputerNameExA, GetAdaptersInfo, MAC formatting with %02x), 0x8560 (GetVersionExW and RtlGetVersion), and 0x8ae0 (GetUserNameA). Discovery thread 0x2200, started by 0x2118 through CreateThread on command 4, runs cmd.exe and wmic.exe command lines through the pipe routine 0x1030. The lines are ipconfig /all, systeminfo, whoami /groups, net view, nltest /domain_trusts, net group 'Domain Admins', net config workstation and the AntiVirusProduct WMI query. The process list is built by 0x1c38 with CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, Process32First and Process32Next. (assessed) [ev_0021, ev_0025, ev_0020, ev_0019]
  11. The beacon formats its report with the template counter=%d&type=%d&guid=%s&os=%d&arch=%d&username=%s&group=%lu&ver=%d.%d&up=%d&direction=%s, encrypts it with RC4 under the key '12345' (0xcbe0, 0xca08, 0xcaac), base64-encodes it (0x6634) and sends it with POST through WinINet (0x4c70, 0x5030) to the decoded C2 URLs. (assessed) [ev_0021, ev_0019, ev_0025, ev_0020]
  12. The reply is base64-decoded, RC4-decrypted and parsed by 0x541c for CLEARURL, URLS and COMMAND tokens. A COMMAND token's numeric id goes to dispatcher 0x3cf4, whose cases cover the discovery, process-list, download-and-run (0xc, 0xd, 0xe, 0x15), self-update (0xf) and exit (0x11) paths. The numeric values on the wire were not observed. (assessed) [ev_0021, ev_0025, ev_0019]
  13. Steady state, assessed: the sandbox recorded no DNS or HTTP session to either decoded C2 name, no command reply, no dropped file and no cmd.exe or wmic.exe child process. The run therefore does not show the beacon's timed loop past its first cycle; that loop is assessed to continue from the code, not observed. (assessed) [ev_0013, ev_0026, ev_0017, ev_0028, ev_0015, ev_0012]

5. Technical analysis · Source per subsection

5.1 Packing, obfuscation and anti-analysis · Source per row

Written by the report model:

No packer is present in the analysed sample. The PE reader reports an empty packer-signature list, and the packer scan matched no packer signature [ev_0004]. The packer scan's only matched rule is HasRichSignature at 0xb8, a Rich header that is a linker artefact, so its packed flag rests on no packing content [ev_0051]. The PE reader reports no overlay and no resources, and the carver found no embedded, split, bitmap-hidden or encrypted PE [ev_0015]. Section entropy is 5.42 (.text), 6.48 (.data), 4.68 (.rdata), 3.87 (.pdata) and 0.08 (.reloc) [ev_0004]. The highest value lies in .data, where the encoded string blobs sit; we assess that this reflects those blobs rather than a packed body [ev_0021]. No YARA rule of the 31 tested matched [ev_0007].

The sample does obfuscate its text. Its strings are stored XOR-encoded in .data and decoded at run time by the routine at 0xae78 [ev_0021]. The routine reads a 4-byte seed, then a 2-byte length XORed with the low 16 bits of the seed, then the encoded bytes. Byte i is XORed with the low byte of (seed + 1 + i). For the seed 0x33629d40 the first key is 0x41, so byte i uses 0x41 + i [ev_0021] [ev_0089]. The blob at 0x10250 shows the layout: header 40 9d 62 33 5f 9d, length 0x9d5f XOR 0x9d40 = 31 bytes (30 characters and a NUL), and a one-step decode yields a URL [ev_0089] [ev_0050]. The platform decoder recovered 101 blobs, and FLOSS emulation recovered 81 strings, 90 of the blobs also being recovered by FLOSS [ev_0021] [ev_0019]. The decoded text includes command-line fragments, WMI and WinINet strings, registry and file paths, and the network values, which are given under command and control [ev_0019] [ev_0021]. The decoder skips encoded text whose bytes already read as printable, so further encoded text may remain undecoded [ev_0021]. The plain-text run list yields 218 runs and the plain-text IOC scan returns no indicators, so the plain strings carry no network or host values [ev_0005] [ev_0006].

API names are hidden as well. The routine at 0x8690 reads the PEB through the GS segment at offset 0x60 [ev_0024], and the resolver at 0x869c parses PE export tables [ev_0008]. The platform's scan recovered 164 Windows function and module names from stored CRC32 values, across 10 hash algorithms [ev_0020]. The CRC-32 routine at 0x6aa4 uses the CRC-32 polynomial constant, and the FNV routine at 0xc99c uses the FNV-32 offset basis and prime [ev_0023] [ev_0008]. The hashing structure is established; the individual name-to-hash mappings are recovered by scan and have moderate confidence until confirmed by emulation. Capa also reports obfuscated stack strings at 0x9484 and an RC4 PRGA routine at 0xcaac [ev_0008]. Capa's T1027 and T1027.005 labels are its rule metadata.

[ev_0004, ev_0005, ev_0006, ev_0007, ev_0008, ev_0015, ev_0019, ev_0020, ev_0021, ev_0023, ev_0024, ev_0050, ev_0051, ev_0089]

The analysed sample carries two evasion-relevant behaviours: obfuscation of its strings and API names, and self-deletion of its own image. The deterministic anti-debug API count is zero, and the Ghidra anti-analysis scan matched only a single PEB read (GS segment, offset 0x60) at 0x8690, which the capa rule also reports as PEB access [ev_0024][ev_0008]. The sample is not packed by any known signature and matched none of the 31 YARA rules run [ev_0004][ev_0007]. The analysed sample is therefore not shown to use anti-debug or environment checks.

Data obfuscation is the main evasion measure. Most strings are stored as XOR-encoded blobs with a rolling 8-bit key (seed 0x33629d40, first key 0x41, UTF-16LE or ASCII text) and are decoded at run time by routine 0xae78, whose body XORs each encoded byte with a key stream advanced per byte [ev_0021][ev_0067]. Emulation recovered 81 decoded strings and the platform decoded 101 blobs [ev_0019][ev_0021]. API names are resolved at run time from CRC-32 hashes: 164 hashed values resolve to Windows functions and modules, and the resolver routines sit at 0x84e4 and 0x904c [ev_0020][ev_0025]. The capa rules report obfuscated stack strings at 0x9484, CRC-32 hashing at 0x6aa4, RC4 PRGA at 0xcaac and FNV hashing at 0xc99c [ev_0008][ev_0023]. Dynamic API resolution (T1027.007) is therefore the sample's main way of hiding its imports, since its import table lists only KERNEL32.dll and USER32.dll [ev_0004].

Self-deletion is implemented in routine 0x3a24. It gets the module path through the GetModuleFileNameW wrapper 0x841c, called from 0x463c [ev_0069]. It opens that path with DELETE access, sets a FILE_RENAME_INFO whose target is the decoded text :wtfbbq (the string at 0xfa18) through SetFileInformationByHandle class 3, closes the handle, reopens the file and sets FileDispositionInfo (class 4) with delete-on-close [ev_0069][ev_0019][ev_0021]. A rename whose target has no base name moves the default data stream into a named alternate data stream on NTFS, so the code removes the running image's directory entry while its content remains in the stream. Routine 0x33ac, the install path, calls 0x3a24 after its copy step and before launching a new process through the CreateProcessW wrapper 0xb400 [ev_0086][ev_0090]. Dispatch case 0xf in 0x3cf4 calls 0x3c2c, 0x3a24 and 0x3808, then starts a fresh copy [ev_0077]. Routine 0x3808 builds the install path through 0x30d0 and 0x3250 and deletes it through the NtDeleteFile wrapper 0x7694 [ev_0098][ev_0025]. The install-path strings are Custom_update, Update_%x, .dll, .exe and AppData [ev_0019].

No file activity was observed in the run. The sandbox reported zero dropped files and no files section, and its observed process was rundll32 running the sample as a DLL with the #1 export [ev_0015][ev_0026][ev_0028]. The rename, the delete and the copy step are therefore established only from code. We assess with high confidence that 0x3a24 renames and then schedules deletion of the running image, and with moderate confidence that it runs in the install and self-update paths. The copy step is inferred: the code shown calls file-creation wrappers from 0x33ac, but the copy destination is not established.

[ev_0004, ev_0007, ev_0008, ev_0015, ev_0019, ev_0020, ev_0021, ev_0023, ev_0024, ev_0025, ev_0026, ev_0028, ev_0067, ev_0069, ev_0077, ev_0086, ev_0090, ev_0098]

Measured: section entropy .text 5.42, .rdata 4.68, .data 6.48, .pdata 3.87, .reloc 0.08.

Measured: capa rules

5.2 API and string resolution · Source per row

Written by the report model:

The sample resolves most of its Windows API surface at run time rather than through its import table. The static import table holds five names from two libraries (KERNEL32: PeekNamedPipe, GetLastError, CreateMutexW; USER32: MessageBeep, MessageBoxA) [ev_0004]. The resolver finds 164 Windows function and module names from stored 32-bit CRC values, across 27 DLLs and 108 module names, using CRC-32 variants: crc32_ascii for API names and crc32_utf16le (with a lower-case variant) for module names [ev_0020]. The hash values sit in tables at 0x84e4 (kernel32 module names), 0x904c (WinINet, urlmon, shell32, advapi32, shlwapi, iphlpapi, user32), 0x9484 (kernel32 process, thread, memory, file and pipe APIs, plus ntdll critical-section calls), 0xa47c (module names), 0xa59c (ntdll Nt* and Rtl APIs and LdrLoadDll) and 0xac50 (ole32) [ev_0020]. The resolution routine at 0x869c walks the export table of a loaded module and calls the CRC-32 routine 0x6aa4 on each export name, returning the first match [ev_0068]. The PEB is read at 0x8690 through the GS segment at offset 0x60 [ev_0024, ev_0008]. Four functions call 0x869c [ev_0025].

The structure is confirmed; the individual values are not. Emulating 0x6aa4 against the CreateMutexW value 0x2d789102 returned no match over five candidates [ev_0091, ev_0093]. The CRC-32 polynomial constant in the file agrees with 0x6aa4 [ev_0023]. The hash-to-name rows are therefore the platform's reading, and each name should be treated as unverified until the hash is reproduced. Two entries are listed under both kernel32.dll and kernelbase.dll, which reflects that the same export is reachable through either module [ev_0020].

The resolved names are wired to call sites through slots. Examples from the per-function index: 0x1c38 and 0x14d8 call CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, Process32First and Process32Next; 0x7fa8 calls GetAdaptersInfo and GetComputerNameExA; 0x8d90 calls InternetOpenW, InternetOpenUrlW and InternetReadFile; 0x8560 calls GetVersionExW and RtlGetVersion; 0x463c calls OpenProcess, ReadProcessMemory and NtQueryInformationProcess; 0x1030 calls CreatePipe, CreateProcessW, PeekNamedPipe and ReadFile [ev_0025]. Several ntdll calls are made through slots without a Win32 wrapper, including NtAllocateVirtualMemory (wrapper 0xad34, 31 callers) and NtFreeVirtualMemory (0x7b40, 41 callers) [ev_0025].

The resolved set spans more behaviour than the imports. By resolved name, the profile is filesystem 22, process_injection 18, execution 15, network 11, discovery 10, anti_debug 7, registry 5, evasion 4 and persistence 4; the import-only read gives persistence 1 [ev_0022, ev_0010]. These are catalogue associations, not observations. The resolved set includes VirtualAllocEx, WriteProcessMemory and CreateRemoteThread, but in the analysed sample's function index no routine calls WriteProcessMemory or CreateRemoteThread through a slot, and the only process-access calls are the OpenProcess, ReadProcessMemory and NtQueryInformationProcess calls in 0x463c [ev_0020, ev_0025].

Strings are stored XOR-encoded in .data and decoded at run time by routine 0xae78. The routine reads a 4-byte seed and a 2-byte length word XORed with the seed's low 16 bits, then XORs each text byte with the low byte of a running value that starts at seed+1 and increases by one per byte. The routine 0x9040 returns its argument plus one, which provides that running value [ev_0067, ev_0068]. With seed 0x33629d40 the first key is 0x41 [ev_0021]. The decoder yields 101 blobs from .data, 90 of which match texts recovered by FLOSS emulation of the same routine [ev_0021, ev_0019]. Static decoding of blob 0xfac0 gives the UTF-16LE text init -zzzz= followed by two %s fields in quotes, 38 bytes long [ev_0088, ev_0021]. FLOSS's rendering of the same call site at 0x4571 omits the zzzz run [ev_0019]. The static decode is taken as the text the routine formats. Plain-text scanning finds no indicators in the 218 ASCII and UTF-16 runs, so the API names, command strings and URLs are reachable only through this decoding or through the hash tables [ev_0005, ev_0006].

[ev_0004, ev_0005, ev_0006, ev_0008, ev_0010, ev_0019, ev_0020, ev_0021, ev_0022, ev_0023, ev_0024, ev_0025, ev_0067, ev_0068, ev_0088, ev_0091, ev_0093]

Measured: 5 static imports from 2 libraries.

Measured: capa rules

5.4 Persistence · Source per row

Written by the report model:

The analysed sample's persistence is a logon-triggered Task Scheduler task named 'Updater', created by the install routine at 0x33ac. Its code is reached from the gate at 0x3868 [ev_0069]. The gate calls 0x378c, which opens a path built by 0x3250 through the NT file-open routine at 0x77b0 and returns whether the open succeeded [ev_0094, ev_0086]. Routine 0x3868 calls 0x33ac only when 0x378c returns zero, so we assess the install branch runs when that open fails. Before that point, 0x3868 applies version and process-count checks through 0x8560 and 0x8820 and exits with -1 when they fail [ev_0069, ev_0082]. The export 'extra' (0x3ce4) calls 0x3cb4, which calls 0x3868 [ev_0081, ev_0077]. The export table lists four names: extra, follower, run and scub [ev_0004, ev_0081].

Routine 0x33ac first copies the module path, held in DAT_1360bc10448 and set through GetModuleFileNameW in 0x841c [ev_0069, ev_0025], to a new file. The destination is built by 0x3250 from the AppData folder (0x7b98 index 0) plus a name from 0x3194 in the 'Update_%x' format and a '.dll' or '.exe' suffix [ev_0086, ev_0077, ev_0021]. The copy routine 0x7504 is called with its delete flag clear, so the source is not removed [ev_0090]. The task name 'Updater' is the decoded string at 0xf988 [ev_0021, ev_0086]. The action program and arguments depend on flag DAT_1360bc10488. When the flag is nonzero, the program is 'rundll32.exe' (0xf9b8) and the arguments are formatted from the copy path (0xf9d8). When it is zero, the program is formatted from 0xf9a0 with the copy path, and the arguments are the literal at 0xf9b0 [ev_0086, ev_0021]. The texts at 0xf9a0, 0xf9b0 and 0xf9d8 are not decoded in the analysed sample's string recovery, so their values are not stated here.

Routine 0x33ac passes name, program and arguments to 0x3728, which passes them to 0xb344 with the user name from GetUserNameA (0x8ae0) [ev_0090, ev_0025]. Routine 0xb344 calls CoInitializeEx and CoCreateInstance through 0xc5e8 [ev_0025, ev_0092] and then 0xc42c [ev_0092]. Routine 0xc42c calls 0xc2c4, which gets the trigger collection and creates a logon trigger (type 9) with the Id 'LogonTrigger' (0x10030) [ev_0069, ev_0021]. It then calls 0xc19c, which sets the exec action's path and arguments [ev_0092]. It calls 0xc7b4, which writes the string 'PT0S' (0x10118) into the task settings [ev_0092, ev_0021]. It calls 0xc8c4 for the principal [ev_0092]. Finally it calls the folder object's vtable slot +0x88 with the path, the definition, flags 6 (create or update), null user and password, and logon type 0 [ev_0077]. We assess slot +0x88 is RegisterTaskDefinition, an inference from the argument pattern and not checked against a type library.

After the task call, 0x33ac starts the command it built through CreateProcessW in 0xb400, with CREATE_NO_WINDOW [ev_0090]. This is an immediate launch and not a persistence step.

No Run-key or Startup-folder write appears in the analysed code. Routine 0x7b98 resolves five folder names through the Shell Folders key using 0xb154, which reads the key through 0x88f8 (RtlFormatCurrentUserKeyPath) and 0xb1d4 (NtQueryValueKey, NtClose) [ev_0069, ev_0098, ev_0025]. NtSetValueKey is among the resolved ntdll names [ev_0020], but no routine calling it appears in the function listings of the analysed sample.

The sandbox observed a rundll32.exe command line naming the sample's .dll with ',#1' [ev_0028]. The sandbox output carries no services, tasks or registry telemetry, so the task registration itself was not observed [ev_0026].

Observed in sandbox: no persistence observed. The evidence is partial: 1 of 149 tool calls failed.

Assessed: persistence the analysts listed (not observed; no count, rule match or detection draft reads it)

5.5 Discovery · Written by the report model

Discovery is carried out by code that runs only after the dispatcher receives a numeric command, and the sandbox run did not exercise it. The static import table holds five entries (PeekNamedPipe, GetLastError, CreateMutexW, MessageBeep, MessageBoxA) and none is a discovery API [ev_0004]. The discovery calls are resolved at run time by CRC32 hash, for example CreateToolhelp32Snapshot at 0x94ef, GetAdaptersInfo at 0x93eb, GetUserNameA at 0x92a3, GetCursorPos at 0x92cc and RtlGetVersion inside the 0xa59c table [ev_0020].

Host command discovery (T1059.003, T1016, T1082, T1033, T1018, T1482, T1069.002, T1518.001). Dispatcher case 4 calls 0x2118, which creates a thread on 0x2200 [ev_0077]. The thread decodes eleven cmd.exe or wmic.exe command pairs with the string decoder 0xae78 [ev_0067, ev_0068]. The decoded texts are: 'ipconfig /all', 'systeminfo', 'nltest /domain_trusts', 'nltest /domain_trusts /all_trusts', 'net view /all /domain', 'net view /all', 'net group "Domain Admins" /domain', a WMIC query for AntiVirusProduct (Get * /Format:List, run with C:\Windows\System32\wbem\wmic.exe), 'net config workstation', a WMIC DisplayName query with a 'No Antivirus installed' fallback, and 'whoami /groups' [ev_0019, ev_0021]. Each pair is passed to 0x1030 [ev_0067]. The routine 0x1030 creates a pipe, starts the process through the slot that the hash resolution fills with CreateProcessW, sets STARTUPINFO flags |= 0x101 with a hidden window, and reads the output back through PeekNamedPipe and ReadFile [ev_0067, ev_0025]. The eleven outputs are stored in beacon fields by 0xb984: &ipconfig=, &systeminfo=, &domain_trusts=, &domain_trusts_all=, &net_view_all_domain=, &net_view_all=, &net_group=, &wmic=, &net_config_ws=, &net_wmic_av= and &whoami_group= [ev_0067, ev_0021]. The combined buffer is cached in DAT_10548 and handed to the beacon as data type 3 [ev_0067]. The code routes these commands to execution: the pipe routine starts cmd.exe with CreateProcessW [ev_0067][ev_0025], but the sandbox recorded only rundll32.exe and svchost.exe processes and no cmd.exe or net child [ev_0012], so execution of the commands was not observed.

Process enumeration (T1057). Dispatcher case 3 calls 0x1c38 [ev_0077]. That routine takes a Toolhelp snapshot through a slot, walks it with Process32FirstW and Process32NextW, and opens the &proclist=[ field [ev_0092]. For each process it calls 0x14d8, which takes a second snapshot, collects child entries under the "subproc" key and recurses on each child PID [ev_0100, ev_0021]. The count routine 0x8820 walks the same snapshot APIs and returns the number of processes [ev_0082].

Registry reads (T1012). Routine 0x7b98 passes the Shell Folders key path (0x10330) and five folder names (AppData, Desktop, Startup, Personal, Local AppData at 0x102a0 to 0x10300) to 0xb154 [ev_0069, ev_0021]. Routine 0xb154 opens the key through 0x88f8, which uses RtlFormatCurrentUserKeyPath, and reads the value through NtQueryValueKey [ev_0098, ev_0086, ev_0025]. The NtOpenKey slot is in 0xb0c4 [ev_0025]. The routine 0x88f8 selects the HKLM root when its first argument is 1 and otherwise builds the current-user path [ev_0086]. The HKLM root text is the decoded literal \Registry\Machine\ at 0x10220 [ev_0021, ev_0019].

Host and identity reads. Routine 0x7fa8 resolves GetComputerNameExA and GetAdaptersInfo, and formats &computername=%s, &mac= and two-digit hex MAC bytes [ev_0082, ev_0025, ev_0021]. Routine 0x8560 maps the OS version reported by RtlGetVersion or GetVersionExW to a value from 0 to 7 [ev_0082]. Routine 0x8ae0 resolves GetUserNameA [ev_0025]. Routine 0x88a4 resolves GetCursorPos and GetTickCount [ev_0025], and routine 0x89d4 resolves FindFirstVolumeW and GetVolumeInformationW [ev_0025].

Desktop listing. Dispatcher case 2 calls 0x1a08 [ev_0077]. That routine calls the SHGetFolderPathA wrapper 0x7f1c with CSIDL 0x10, enumerates the folder with FindFirstFileA and FindNextFileA, skips the '.' and '..' entries, and writes the names under &desklinks=[ [ev_0092, ev_0025, ev_0021].

The C2 reply handler 0x541c parses the numeric command that reaches the dispatcher [ev_0082]. No reply that carries a discovery command was observed in the sandbox run; the reply parser passes a COMMAND id to the dispatcher [ev_0082], and the numeric ids on the wire were not observed, so the trigger for this routine is not established [ev_0077].

0xc download EXE and run The dispatcher rewrites the argument first. If it is longer than 8 characters and begins with the decoded 'front' prefix (case-insensitive), it is copied up to its first single slash, the decoded '/files/' is appended, and the rest of the argument follows [ev_0077, ev_0021]. It then calls 0x6e04 with the argument as the URL and launch flag 1. That routine formats '%s%d.exe' with the folder from 0x7e90 (CSIDL 0x1c, Local AppData), fetches the URL over WinINet through 0x6f48, and writes the body to that file through 0x7acc (NtCreateFile and NtWriteFile). It starts the file with CreateProcessW through 0xb400 when the body is non-empty. The write is gated by a prefix compare against '<html>' and '<!DOCTYPE' (0xbcac), whose outcome is not fully resolved [ev_0069, ev_0067, ev_0068, ev_0021]. ev_0077, ev_0069, ev_0067, ev_0068, ev_0021

The routine formats '%s%d.dll' under the 0x1c shell folder, fetches the URL through 0x6f48, and writes the body with the same 0x7acc path as 0xc. With launch set and a non-empty body, it calls 0xb4cc, which formats 'C:\WINDOWS\SYSTEM32\rundll32.exe %s,%s' with the file and the export name and starts it with CreateProcessW [ev_0069, ev_0067, ev_0021, ev_0025]. The prefix rewrite applies as in 0xc. ev_0077, ev_0069, ev_0067, ev_0021, ev_0025
0xe download into memory and run on a thread The dispatcher calls 0x7170 with the argument as URL. The routine fetches the body through 0x6f48 with no destination, so nothing is written to disk. It allocates a region with VirtualAlloc (size from the body, protection 0x40, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE), copies the body into it, and starts thread 0xb398 on that region with CreateThread [ev_0081, ev_0067]. The prefix rewrite applies as in 0xc. ev_0077, ev_0081, ev_0067
0xf self-update The dispatcher calls 0x6e04 with launch flag 0 to fetch the argument into '%s%d.exe' without starting it. It then calls 0x3c2c, which closes stored handles, calls 0x3a24, which renames the module's default data stream to ':wtfbbq' and sets delete-on-close, and calls 0x3808, which builds the path and calls the NtDeleteFile wrapper 0x7694. It then calls 0x6e04 with launch flag 1 to start the fresh copy and calls the ExitProcess slot with 0 [ev_0077, ev_0069, ev_0098, ev_0025]. The prefix rewrite applies as in 0xc. ev_0077, ev_0069, ev_0098, ev_0025
0x11 exit The dispatcher calls the ExitProcess slot (_DAT_1360bc10b68) with 0 and ends the process [ev_0077]. ev_0077
0x12 second-stage fetch and launch (bp.dat) The dispatcher rewrites the argument with the 'front' prefix rule as in 0xc, then calls 0x4110 with the argument. That routine builds a URL with the decoded '%s%s' format and the 'files/bp.dat' name, fetches it through 0x6f48, names the saved copy with '%s%d.dll' and '%d.dat' formats, and launches the DLL through the rundll32 launcher 0xb4cc with the decoded argument string 'init -zzzz=' [ev_0077, ev_0021, ev_0019]. The exact construction of the URL is not fully traced. ev_0077, ev_0021, ev_0019
0x13 long-sleep flag The dispatcher sets DAT_1360bc1058c to 1. The beacon loop in 0x5750 then draws its sleep from ((rand % 600) + 0x5dc) * 1000 ms, which is 1500 to 2099 seconds, instead of 450 to 599 seconds [ev_0077, ev_0067]. ev_0077, ev_0067
0x14 counter reset flag The dispatcher sets DAT_1360bc10590 to 1. The beacon loop in 0x5750 then resets its counter DAT_1360bc10588 to 0 on its next cycle [ev_0077, ev_0067]. ev_0077, ev_0067
0x15 download into memory and run in a mapped section The dispatcher calls 0x72c8 with the argument as URL. The routine fetches the body through 0x6f48 with no destination and starts thread 0xb670 with the body pointer. That thread creates a section with CreateFileMappingA and maps it with MapViewOfFile, copies the body in, and calls through the body pointer [ev_0081, ev_0086]. 5.7 Command and control · Source per row

Written by the report model:

The sample's C2 is HTTP(S), built around two candidate endpoints. Its decoded strings are hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ and hxxps://skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/ [ev_0019] [ev_0021] [ev_0050]. Routine 0x6988 decodes both into a two-entry table at DAT_1360bc10568, and the table is filled from the install routine 0x3868 [ev_0067]. The beacon thread 0x5750 is started by CreateThread from 0x6250 at the end of 0x3868, and only when the gate in 0x3868 succeeds [ev_0069]. The beacon cycles through the table, cracking each entry with InternetCrackUrlA (0x52c4) and moving to the next entry on failure [ev_0067] [ev_0082].

Transport is WinINet, with every API resolved by hash [ev_0020]. Routine 0x4c70 calls InternetOpenW and InternetConnectA with service type 3 (HTTP) [ev_0082] [ev_0025]. Routine 0x5030 calls HttpOpenRequestA, HttpSendRequestA and InternetSetOptionA [ev_0069] [ev_0025]. It selects the verb 'POST' (0xfba0) when its second argument is nonzero and 'GET' (0xfbb0) otherwise, and it sends the Content-Type header 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded' (0xfb68) [ev_0021] [ev_0069]. The user agent is 'Mozilla/4.0 (compatible; MSIE 7.0; Windows NT 5.1; Tob 1.1)' (0xfbe0 and 0xfc60), used by 0x4d20 [ev_0021] [ev_0090]. For an HTTPS URL, 0x5030 sets option 0x1f to 0x4380 [ev_0069]. The reply is read with InternetReadFile in 255-byte reads by 0x4f58 [ev_0098].

Each report is built by 0x5750 from the template 'counter=%d&type=%d&guid=%s&os=%d&arch=%d&username=%s&group=%lu&ver=%d.%d&up=%d&direction=%s' [ev_0019] [ev_0067]. The guid argument is supplied by routine 0x7b80, whose body calls the volume routine 0x89d4 and the routine 0x8bbc [ev_0098]. The group value is the FNV hash of the decoded text 'Littlehw' [ev_0019] [ev_0021] [ev_0106]; routine 0x463c calls the FNV routine 0xc99c on that text [ev_0069]. Host fields are appended as '&computername=%s', '&domain=%s', '&mac=%02x' and '&ipconfig=' [ev_0019] [ev_0021] [ev_0025]. Discovery output is appended as '&systeminfo=', '&proclist=[' and '&desklinks=[' [ev_0019] [ev_0021] [ev_0025]. The process-list JSON uses '"pid": ' and '"proc": ' keys [ev_0019] [ev_0025]. The report body is RC4-encrypted with the key text '12345' (0xfd30) through 0xcbe0 and 0xcaac, then base64-encoded by 0x6634 before the POST [ev_0021] [ev_0067] [ev_0086] [ev_0090]. The reply is base64-decoded (0x6370), RC4-decrypted with the same key and passed to the parser 0x541c [ev_0082] [ev_0067].

The parser 0x541c splits the decrypted reply into tokens and compares them against the decoded keywords 'CLEARURL', 'URLS' and 'COMMAND' [ev_0019] [ev_0021] [ev_0082]. 'CLEARURL' clears the URL table (0xce20), 'URLS' with an index and a URL stores the entry (0xccbc), and 'COMMAND' with a numeric id and arguments calls the dispatcher 0x3cf4 [ev_0082] [ev_0090]. [cited to an entry that does not hold it: "CLEARURL", "URLS" are not in ev_0082, ev_0090; 3 other entries hold them; "COMMAND" is not in ev_0082, ev_0090; 10 other entries hold it] The dispatcher's switch covers ids 2, 3, 4, 0xc, 0xd, 0xe, 0xf, 0x11, 0x12, 0x13, 0x14 and 0x15, mapping to the listing, process list, discovery thread, download-and-run and update routines [ev_0077]. The numeric ids as sent on the wire were not observed. After a parse, 0xd1c8 writes the URL table lines 'URLS|%d|%s' encrypted with the DAT_10458 key to the configuration file 'update_data.dat' [ev_0098] [ev_0021].

Beacon timing, from the 0x5750 listing: 1800 iterations of 100 ms (about 180 s) before the first cycle, then a sleep of (rand % 150 + 450) seconds, or (rand % 600 + 1500) seconds when flag DAT_1058c is set [ev_0067].

Observed behaviour: none of this was observed on the wire. The sandbox network section lists two hosts, one a DNS resolver and one a TCP peer, with no HTTP entries and no DNS lookups for the C2 names [ev_0013] [ev_0026]. The sandbox does not attribute these flows to a process of the sample [ev_0013]. The capture holds 1,474 packets and 14 external conversations, none attributed to the sample, and no DNS answer or TLS SNI for either candidate host [ev_0017] [ev_0115] [ev_0117]. Its HTTP GETs go to Windows update, OCSP and certificate-revocation hosts, with no POST [ev_0116]. The TCP peer carries a domain tag for a certificate-revocation host and is not tied to a C2 role [ev_0103]. The gate 0x3868 can stop the run before any beacon is sent [ev_0069]. We assess, with moderate confidence, that the two decoded URLs are the sample's configured C2 endpoints, and that the sandbox run did not establish contact with them.

5.8 Payloads and dropped files · Written by the report model

No payload is carved from the file. The carver returned zero payloads, the PE has no overlay and no embedded or split PE, and the parsed resource table is empty [ev_0004]. The sandbox recorded 0 dropped files and no files section [ev_0015][ev_0026], so no second-stage file was observed at run time.

The encoded material in .data is the sample's own text, not a payload. Its decoded blobs (101 texts, 90 also recovered by FLOSS) include the file-name formats the code builds: "%s%d.dll" (0xffe0), "%s%d.exe" (0x10018), "%s%d.dll" (0xfa70), "%d.dat" (0xfa90), "Update_%x" (0xf920), "Custom_update" (0xf940), "\update_data.dat" (0x103b8) and "files/bp.dat" (0xfa58) [ev_0019][ev_0021][ev_0025]. The blob at 0x10250 decodes to the URL hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ [ev_0021]. These formats name files the code would write; no file body is stored in the image.

The code paths that would create files are as follows, read from the decompiled routines and not observed in a run:

  • Download routine 0x6f48 fetches a body through 0x8d90 (WinINet). With a path argument it rejects bodies that begin with "<html>" or "<!DOCTYPE" and writes the body through 0x7acc, which opens the file with NtCreateFile (wrapper 0x77b0, disposition 3) and writes it with NtWriteFile (0x7a54). With no path it returns before any write [ev_0067][ev_0068][ev_0081]. Whether the write branch is reached depends on the return sense of 0xbcac, which is not established.
  • Routine 0x6e04 builds a "%s%d.exe" name [ev_0021], calls 0x6f48 [ev_0069], and when its launch flag is set starts the file with CreateProcessW through 0xb400 [ev_0069][ev_0090].
  • Routine 0x6c2c builds a "%s%d.dll" name, calls 0x6f48, and when its flag is set launches the file through 0xb4cc. That routine formats the command line "C:\WINDOWS\SYSTEM32\rundll32.exe %s,%s" and calls CreateProcessW [ev_0069][ev_0021].
  • Routine 0x4110 (the "files/bp.dat" stage) calls 0x6f48 and then 0xb4cc with the argument string "init -zzzz="%s%s"" (0xfac0) [ev_0077][ev_0021].
  • Routines 0x7170 and 0x72c8 fetch a body with no path, copy it into a VirtualAlloc buffer with protection 0x40, and run it on a new thread (0xb398 and 0xb670). No file is written on that path [ev_0081][ev_0086].
  • Routine 0x33ac copies the running image into %APPDATA%\Custom_update\Update_<hex>.<ext> through 0x7504 and 0x7acc, then starts the copy with CreateProcessW [ev_0086][ev_0090][ev_0021]. Routine 0x3808 deletes the installed copy [ev_0098].

In the sandbox, two processes carried the command line rundll32.exe with the sample's own path and the export ordinal #1, and a third was svchost.exe -k GPSvcGroup [ev_0012][ev_0028]. We assess the rundll32 launch is the sandbox harness's invocation, since the sample's code builds its rundll32 command from the 0xfe90 format and the name formats above, not from a path it names itself [ev_0028]. The capture's only file GETs are Windows update CAB files and certificate revocation fetches [ev_0017]; no GET for a sample-named file appears.

Confidence that the sample writes and runs downloaded files, as shown in code: high. Confidence that any file was written or run during the sandbox run: none; the run recorded no such file.

Command and Control (TA0011) T1105 Ingress Tool Transfer The sample has code paths that fetch a body over WinINet and either write it to disk and run it, or run it from memory. Execution is not shown. - Disk path: codes 0xd, 0xc and 0xf in the dispatcher at 0x3cf4 reach 0x6c2c (code 0xd) and 0x6e04 (codes 0xc and 0xf). Each builds a destination "<shell folder><n>.dll" or ".exe" through the shell-folder routine 0x7e90 and calls 0x6f48 with the URL from its command argument. The disk branch of 0x6f48 calls 0x7acc, which uses 0x77b0 (NtCreateFile) and 0x7a54 (NtWriteFile). 0x6c2c then runs the file through 0xb4cc, which builds "C:\WINDOWS\SYSTEM32\rundll32.exe %s,%s" and calls CreateProcessW; 0x6e04 runs it through 0xb400 (CreateProcessW). - Memory path: 0x7170 (code 0xe) and 0x72c8 (code 0x15) call 0x6f48 with no destination, so the body stays in memory. 0x7170 copies it into a VirtualAlloc region and starts 0xb398 on a thread, which calls into that region. 0x72c8 starts 0xb670, which maps a section and calls through the body pointer. - Caveats: the decompile's comparison of the body against "<html>" and "<!DOCTYPE" (in 0x6f48) uses an output-slot operand, so what exactly it tests is uncertain. The URL and export name arrive as command arguments. The static read does not show their content, so whether the fetch targets the C2 candidates in the IOC claim is not established. - The dispatcher 0x3cf4 is called only by 0x541c, which refers to the decoded COMMAND, URLS and CLEARURL texts.; rule fetching a file over HTTP or FTP straight to disk (api_capability), matched only names resolved at runtime from hashes, no import: InternetReadFile, URLDownloadToFileW; base rate fires on 0.0% of benign software (0 of 2730 freely distributed Windows binaries from 25 independent vendors); 0 held-out malware profiles support it api_capability (rule match on names resolved at runtime from hashes only, no import; not counted as corroboration), all_tools_qu1cksc0pe, all_tools_reverser_ghidra, all_tools_static_r2, dynamic, network, static, triage, judge high confidence, 0.90, stated by the all_tools_reverser_ghidra analyst published, corroborated (named by 6 analyst layers, each in a statement of its own; their statements are listed below the table; 33 evidence roots, and 11 citations whose root could not be read); unresolved: attck.claim_does_not_describe ev_0015, ev_0026, ev_0025, ev_0067, ev_0068, ev_0081, ev_0069, ev_0021, ev_0078, ev_0022
Command and Control (TA0011) T1573.001 Symmetric Cryptography [Static] The beacon encrypts its traffic with RC4. Verdict: CONFIRMED (code). Routine 0x5750 builds the report, RC4-encrypts it under "12345" (0xcbe0 then the wrapper 0xc964, which calls PRGA 0xcaac), base64-encodes it (0x6634), POSTs it (0x4d20), base64-decodes the reply (0x6370), RC4-decrypts it with the same key, and passes the text to the command parser 0x541c. The key schedule 0xca08 (j += S[i] + K[i mod n], then swap) and the PRGA 0xcaac (i++, j += S[i], swap, XOR with S[(S[i]+S[j]) & 0xff]) are the standard RC4 algorithm. all_tools_reverser_ghidra, dynamic, network, judge moderate-to-high confidence, 0.85, stated by the all_tools_reverser_ghidra analyst published, corroborated (named by 3 analyst layers, each in a statement of its own; 15 evidence roots, and 3 citations whose root could not be read) -

Defense Evasion (TA0005) T1027.005 Indicator Removal from Tools rule contain obfuscated stackstrings (capa), namespace anti-analysis/obfuscation/string/stackstring; base rate not measured capa (rule match) rule match not published: a rule matched it and no producer claimed it ev_0008

Techniques a rule matched only on names resolved at runtime from hashes (the match is not counted as corroboration; each analyst statement naming the technique follows, verbatim):

  • T1055 Process Injection:
    • dynamic: Write and remote-thread APIs resolved by hash; catalogue rule fires on the set (benign 0.5%); no routine slot-calls WriteProcessMemory or CreateRemoteThread in the index.
  • T1070.004 File Deletion:
    • triage: The sample deletes itself by rename and delete-on-close. Dispatch code 0xf in 0x3cf4 calls 0x3a24, which opens the module path with CreateFileW and calls SetFileInformationByHandle twice: once with class 3 (rename) and a buffer holding the stream name, then with class 4 (disposition, delete on close) and value 1. The stream name ":wtfbbq" (decoded at 0xfa18) is decoded inside 0x3a24 and used as the rename target, which is an alternate data stream of the module.
    • all_tools_qu1cksc0pe: After installing or updating, the sample deletes its own file. It opens the module with CreateFileW and calls SetFileInformationByHandle with the ":wtfbbq" stream name, which renames the default stream to an alternate stream, and then sets the delete disposition.
    • static: The sample deletes its own file. The routine 0x3a24 targets the file at DAT_1360bc10448, which the code fills from GetModuleFileNameW (through 0x841c, called from 0x463c). 0x3a24 opens the file, sets a FileRenameInfo (class 3) that renames the default data stream to ":wtfbbq", closes it, reopens it, and sets FileDispositionInfo (class 4, delete on close). It is called from 0x33ac and from the command-0xf path in 0x3cf4.
    • static: The install path runs in 0x33ac, which the main routine 0x3868 calls when 0x378c returns 0. 0x33ac calls the scheduled-task path 0x3728, the self-delete 0x3a24, the file-creation wrappers 0x7588 (NtCreateFile and NtClose), and CreateProcessW through 0xb400. It exits with ExitProcess. The sequence is most likely: copy self to an install location, register the task, delete the original, and run the installed copy. Only the copy and run steps are inferred. The "rundll32.exe" string (0xf9b8) is referenced here too.
    • static: 0x3a24 renames the default data stream of the module's own file to ':wtfbbq' and then sets delete-on-close. It is called from 0x33ac and from the command-0xf path.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Self-deletion] Routine 0x3a24 opens its own image path with DELETE access, renames the file to the NTFS alternate data stream ":wtfbbq" (SetFileInformationByHandle class 3, FileRenameInfo), closes the handle, reopens it, and sets delete-on-close (class 4, FileDispositionInfo). It is called by 0x33ac after the copy and before the relaunch, and by the dispatcher case 0xf.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] Routine 0x3808 deletes the installed copy. It builds the install path with 0x30d0 (AppData through 0x7b98(0), the folder "Custom_update" and the volume-derived "Update_%x" name, claim 72) and 0x3250 (the .dll or .exe extension). It normalises the path (0x6b9c) and deletes it through the NtDeleteFile wrapper 0x7694 (index slots NtDeleteFile and RtlInitUnicodeString). It returns 1 on success. Its caller is the self-update case 0xf of 0x3cf4, after the self-rename (0x3a24) and before the relaunch. The sequence is: download the new copy (0x6e04 without launch), close handles (0x3c2c), rename the running image to the stream ":wtfbbq" and set delete-on-close (0x3a24, claim 43), delete the old install copy (0x3808), and start the new copy (0x6e04 with launch). The argument 0x3808 receives is passed to 0x30d0 and 0x3250, which ignore it, so the deleted path is the install path whatever the argument. This is deletion of the sample's own copy (T1070.004).
    • dynamic: The sample deletes its own original image. Routine 0x3a24 opens the image with DELETE access, renames it to the NTFS stream ":wtfbbq" (decoded at 0xfa18) through SetFileInformationByHandle, and sets delete-on-close. The install routine 0x33ac calls 0x3a24 after the copy and before the relaunch. Dispatcher case 0xf calls 0x3a24 and then 0x3808 (deletion of the installed copy) before launching a fresh copy. The body of 0x3a24 is an upstream reading. The sandbox recorded no file activity.
    • dynamic: Install-copy and self-deletion strings. The sample uses "Custom_update" (@0x312f), "Update_%x" (@0x31f5), ".dll" (@0x32a7), ".exe" (@0x32df) and "AppData" (@0x7bd2). It also decodes ":wtfbbq" (@0xfa18), which the upstream reading uses as an NTFS alternate-data-stream rename of the running image followed by delete-on-close in 0x3a24. The sandbox recorded no dropped files and no files section, so neither the copy nor the rename is observed.
    • dynamic: ':wtfbbq' decoded; Custom_update, Update_%x, .dll/.exe, AppData strings; no files observed.
  • T1012 Query Registry:
    • all_tools_qu1cksc0pe: The sample reads the user's Shell Folders registry key through a path that reaches the registry-key routine 0x88f8, which resolves RtlFormatCurrentUserKeyPath. The registry-query APIs NtOpenKey and NtQueryValueKey are resolved and called through slots in 0xb0c4 and 0xb1d4. So the sample reads registry values, probably to find folder paths. Whether those two routines are on that path is not confirmed.
    • static: The sample reads the Shell Folders registry key for folder paths. 0x7b98 holds five folder names ("AppData", "Desktop", "Startup", "Personal", "Local AppData") and resolves them through the Shell Folders key with 0xb154. The registry path passes through 0x88f8 (RtlFormatCurrentUserKeyPath), 0xb0c4 (NtOpenKey) and 0xb1d4 (NtQueryValueKey). No Run-key or RunOnce write is shown. NtSetValueKey is resolved, but its caller is not in the listings.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Registry reads for folder paths] Routine 0x7b98 reads folder names from the Shell Folders key through 0xb154, and 0x88f8 builds the registry path (HKLM \Registry\Machine\ or HKCU through RtlFormatCurrentUserKeyPath). The NtOpenKey and NtQueryValueKey slots are in 0xb0c4 and 0xb1d4. Only index 0 (AppData) is used by callers.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] Routine 0xb154 reads one registry value. It opens a key through 0x88f8, which selects a root from its first argument (HKLM for 1; otherwise the current user, through RtlFormatCurrentUserKeyPath). It appends the sub-key text with 0xba98 (body not read) and reads the value with 0xb1d4 (NtQueryValueKey, per its index row). Its caller on the reached path is 0x7b98, which reads the shell-folder names from "Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Explorer\Shell Folders" with index 0 (AppData) only (claim 46). This is a registry read (T1012).
  • T1057 Process Discovery:
    • triage: Process enumeration is resolved by hash: CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, Process32First and Process32Next (and their W variants) in the functions at 0x9484, 0x1c38, 0x14d8 and 0x8820.
    • all_tools_qu1cksc0pe: The sample lists the running processes with CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, Process32First and Process32Next (routine 0x1c38 builds the list, and routine 0x8820 counts processes) and sends the list to the C2 as the "&proclist=[" field in JSON with "pid" and "proc" keys.
    • static: Process enumeration is resolved by hash: CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, Process32First/Next and their W variants, in functions 0x1c38, 0x14d8 and 0x8820. The sample lists running processes.
    • static: Process enumeration is part of the beacon's data. The routine 0x1c38 lists processes through CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, Process32First and Process32Next (hash-resolved), and the JSON "&proclist=[" and ""proc": " fields hold the result. It runs when the dispatcher receives command 3.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] Process listing: 0x1c38 lists processes with CreateToolhelp32Snapshot and Process32First/Next, and writes a JSON list under "&proclist=[". Verdict: CONFIRMED. Each process gets an entry, and child processes are gathered through 0x14d8 and 0x21a8 ("subproc").
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Command dispatch, switch] Routine 0x3cf4 is the command dispatcher. Its only caller is 0x541c (ev_0078), and it receives a numeric id that 0x541c parses (claim 69). Cases: 2 calls 0x1a08 (Desktop listing, "&desklinks=[", with SHGetFolderPathA through the wrapper 0x7f1c); 3 calls 0x1c38 (process list, claim 16, process discovery T1057); 4 starts the discovery thread (claim 31); 0xc calls 0x6e04 and 0xd calls 0x6c2c, both with launch (0xc runs the downloaded EXE directly; 0xd runs the downloaded DLL through rundll32, T1218.011); 0xe calls 0x7170 and 0x15 calls 0x72c8, both of which run downloaded code in memory. The four download-and-run cases 0xc, 0xd, 0xe and 0x15 are ingress tool transfer (T1105; claims 19 and 50). 0xf is self-update (claim 67). 0x11 exits; 0x12 calls 0x4110 (the bp.dat stage); 0x13 and 0x14 set the long-sleep and counter-reset flags. Before the switch, ids 0x12, 0xe, 0xc, 0xd, 0xf, 4 and 0x15 have their argument rewritten (claim 68).
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] Routine 0x14d8 lists the descendant processes of one PID for the process report. The list builder 0x1c38 calls it for each process, passing that process's PID (ev_0092). It takes a Toolhelp snapshot (slot 0x10b30), walks it with Process32FirstW and Process32NextW (slots 0x10b50 and 0x10b60, PROCESSENTRY32W size 0x130), and for each entry whose parent PID equals the argument it writes a JSON child object. The child's PID and executable name are formatted with two decoded templates, inside the ""proc": " fragment (0xf810) and the ""subproc": [" block (0xf830). It then recurses on the child's PID. 0x21a8, called just before it by 0x1c38, sets the +4 flag on the entry whose PID matches. The flag's use is in 0x2154 (unread), so its effect on the listing is not confirmed.
    • dynamic: The sample enumerates running processes. Routine 0x1c38 takes a Toolhelp snapshot (CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, slot 0x10b30), walks it with Process32FirstW and Process32NextW, and writes a JSON list under "&proclist=[" (0xf858). Routine 0x14d8 adds child processes (""subproc": [", 0xf830). Dispatcher case 3 calls 0x1c38, and the C2 reply parser reaches case 3. The sandbox did not show a command for case 3 being sent.
    • dynamic: The sample enumerates processes. CreateToolhelp32Snapshot, Process32First and Process32Next (and W variants) are resolved in the 0x9484 table (CreateToolhelp32Snapshot @0x94ef). Routines 0x1c38 and 0x14d8 call them through slots, and the beacon fields "&proclist=[" (0xf858) and ""proc": " (0xf810) sit beside them.
    • dynamic: Toolhelp snapshot APIs resolved; routines 0x1c38 and 0x14d8 call them; &proclist= field decoded.
    • network: The decoded beacon report carries the computer name, OS version, adapter MAC addresses, user name and the process list (the "&proclist=[" field, sent as data type 4). These are code-level collection capabilities and were not observed. The report travels over the HTTP channel in claim 5, and no separate exfiltration channel is asserted, so T1048 is not used. The techniques are the host and OS read (T1082), the adapter read (T1016), the user read (T1033) and the process listing (T1057).
    • network: Report fields for computer name, MAC, OS, user name, adapter config and discovery output (systeminfo, whoami, process list, AV query). Routing is shown; execution and sending are not.
  • T1071.001 Web Protocols:
    • triage: The file contains an HTTP beacon. The decoded strings include a form-style report with fields for counter, type, guid, OS, architecture, username, group, version, uptime and direction, plus "POST", "GET" and the Content-Type header. The WinINet functions InternetConnectA, InternetOpenW, HttpOpenRequestA, HttpSendRequestA and InternetReadFile are resolved by hash in the functions that build and send it (0x4c70, 0x5030, 0x8d90, 0x5750). The report's contents are not themselves a discovery finding; the host-reading routines are in CLAIM 43.
    • all_tools_qu1cksc0pe: The beacon is sent to the C2 over HTTP. The sample opens a WinINet request (HttpOpenRequestA, HttpSendRequestA) with the verb "POST", a form-urlencoded Content-Type and the "Tob 1.1" user-agent string, to the decoded C2 URLs (titnovacrion[.]top/live/ and skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/), and it reads the reply through InternetReadFile.
    • static: Candidate C2 URLs are decoded from .data: hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ (blob 0x10250, FLOSS at 0x69b3) and hxxps://skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/ (blob 0x10278, FLOSS at 0x6a1f). Both are referenced from the function at 0x6988, which passes them to 0xbc1c. The user agent "Mozilla/4.0 (compatible; MSIE 7.0; Windows NT 5.1; Tob 1.1)" (FLOSS 0x4e47) and the group string "Littlehw" (FLOSS 0x46b1) also decode. The sandbox observed no contact with either domain, so the use of these URLs as C2 is not shown. The HTTP C2 technique is asserted only as a candidate, with that doubt.
    • static: The file contains HTTP beacon machinery. Decoded strings include the report format "counter=%d&type=%d&guid=%s&os=%d&arch=%d&username=%s&group=%lu&ver=%d.%d&up=%d&direction=%s", "&computername=%s", "POST", "GET" and a Content-Type header (decoded at 0xfd40, 0xfb68, 0xfba0, 0xfbb0). The WinINet functions are resolved by hash and called from 0x4c70 (InternetConnectA, InternetOpenW), 0x5030 (HttpOpenRequestA, HttpSendRequestA, InternetSetOptionA) and 0x8d90 (InternetOpenW, InternetOpenUrlW, InternetReadFile). The beacon thread at 0x5750 is started by CreateThread from 0x6250. This is static evidence only; the sandbox shows no HTTP session.
    • static: The beacon thread 0x5750 runs on a fixed schedule with jitter. It waits 1800 × 100 ms (about 180 s) before its first cycle. Each later cycle sleeps (rand % 150 + 450) seconds, which is 450–599 s, or 1500–2099 s when flag DAT_1058c is set. The jitter source is 0x88a4 (GetCursorPos and GetTickCount). Its HTTP helpers are 0x4c70, 0x5030 (HttpOpenRequestA, HttpSendRequestA, POST) and 0x8d90 (InternetOpenW, InternetReadFile).
    • static: The C2 candidate URLs are loaded at start-up. The routine 0x6988 decodes hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ (0x10250) and hxxps://skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/ (0x10278) into a two-entry table at DAT_1360bc10568. The table is filled from 0x3868. Contact is not established.
    • static: Beacon 0x5750 sleeps 450–599 s with jitter (1500–2099 s under a flag), posts through WinINet, parses responses in 0x541c, and dispatches to 0x3cf4, which covers discovery, process listing, download-and-run and an update-and-replace path.
    • static: 0x6988 decodes two candidate C2 URLs into a table used from 0x3868. Contact not established.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] The decoded hosts titnovacrion[.]top and skinnyjeanso[.]com are C2 endpoints that the beacon uses. Verdict: REFINED. Routine 0x6988 decodes both URLs into a two-entry table, and the beacon loop 0x5750 indexes that table round-robin and cracks each entry with InternetCrackUrlA (0x52c4) before posting. Contact is not observed: the sandbox saw no DNS or HTTP to either name.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] The beacon thread 0x5750 is started by CreateThread in 0x6250, and it posts HTTP requests with a form-style report. Verdict: CONFIRMED (code). 0x6250 calls CreateThread with 0x5750 as the entry, and 0x3868 calls 0x6250 at its end. 0x5030 selects the verb "POST" (0xfba0) when its second argument is nonzero and "GET" (0xfbb0) otherwise, then calls HttpOpenRequestA and HttpSendRequestA through slots, with InternetSetOptionA. The report is formatted with the decoded wsprintfA template "counter=%d&type=%d&guid=%s&os=%d&arch=%d&username=%s&group=%lu&ver=%d.%d&up=%d&direction=%s". Whether any request reached a server is not established.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Command dispatch, parser] The reply from the C2 is decrypted and split into lines and tokens by 0x541c, which is carried over the HTTP session (claim 11). Tokens: CLEARURL (decoded 0xfcf0) calls 0xce20, which clears the URL table; URLS (0xfd00) with an index and a URL calls 0xccbc, which stores the URL at that index; COMMAND (0xfd10) with a numeric id and arguments calls the dispatcher 0x3cf4 with that id. After the loop, 0xd1c8 formats "URLS|%d|%s" lines as a reply. 0x541c is called only by the beacon 0x5750.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Beacon request chain] The beacon cracks the URL with 0x52c4 (InternetCrackUrlA), posts through 0x4d20 and 0x5030, reads the reply through 0x4f58 (InternetReadFile) and, on failure, rotates to the next table entry. When the scheme is HTTPS, 0x5030 receives the cracked scheme (value 4), sets the secure flags and calls InternetSetOptionA with security flags 0x4380. The HTTPS channel is an encrypted channel under the shared key of claim 8.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] Routine 0x4c70 (FUN_1360bc04c70) opens a WinINet HTTP connection and returns its handle. Its arguments are the user-agent text (RCX), the server name (RDX) and the port (R8W); R9D is saved and then overwritten (ev_0097). It calls InternetOpenW through slot 0x10e00 with the agent and zeros for the other four arguments (ev_0097, call at 0x4cb0). It then calls InternetConnectA through slot 0x10e08 with the session handle (RCX), the server name (RDX), the port (R8W), a NULL user name (R9), a NULL password (stack 0x20), service 3, INTERNET_SERVICE_HTTP (stack 0x28), flags 0 (stack 0x30) and context 1 (stack 0x38) (ev_0097, call at 0x4cfa). The decompiler listing shows only the first argument of that call, which is why the server and port looked unresolved; the disassembly carries them. Its only caller is 0x4d20 (ev_0090), which passes one of two copies of "Mozilla/4.0 (compatible; MSIE 7.0; Windows NT 5.1; Tob 1.1)" (0xfbe0 and 0xfc60, ev_0021). The server name and port that 0x4d20 places in RDX and R8W at the call are not read here. The handle goes to the request routine 0x5030 (claim 11). The reply is read by 0x4f58 (claim 66).
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] Routine 0x4f58 reads an HTTP reply body. It loops on InternetReadFile (slot 0x10e30) with 255-byte reads into a 256-byte buffer and appends each non-empty chunk to the output block with 0xb058 (body not read). It stops when the read returns FALSE or zero bytes, returns 1 at the end, and returns 0 if an append fails. Its caller is 0x4d20, with the request handle. The bytes read are the C2 reply, which 0x541c decrypts and parses (claim 29).
    • dynamic: The sample reads its C2 reply and parses commands from it. Routine 0x541c decrypts the reply and splits it into tokens: CLEARURL (decoded at 0xfcf0), URLS (0xfd00) and COMMAND (0xfd10). A COMMAND token's numeric id is passed to the dispatcher 0x3cf4, which runs the discovery, download and self-update cases. No command reply was observed in the sandbox.
    • dynamic: The sample is configured with HTTP(S) C2 URLs and sends form-style HTTP POSTs. The extracted configuration names hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ and hxxps://skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/ (decoded at 0x10250 and 0x10278; FLOSS at 0x69b3 and 0x6a1f). WinINet functions are resolved by hash (InternetOpenW at 0x90b7, InternetConnectA at 0x90e0, HttpOpenRequestA at 0x9109, HttpSendRequestA at 0x9132) and called in 0x4c70, 0x5030 and 0x8d90. The verb "POST" (0x5104), a Content-Type header (0x507a) and a report template (0x5c02) are decoded. No request reached a server in the sandbox: the HTTP and DNS lists are empty.
    • dynamic: Configured C2 endpoints and HTTP(S) beacon capability. The extracted configuration lists two C2 URLs (hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ and hxxps://skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/), the group "Littlehw" and the user agent "Mozilla/4.0 (compatible; MSIE 7.0; Windows NT 5.1; Tob 1.1)". The URLs are decoded from the blobs at 0x10250 and 0x10278. The WinINet functions are resolved by hash (InternetOpenW @0x90b7, InternetConnectA @0x90e0, HttpOpenRequestA @0x9109, HttpSendRequestA @0x9132). The beacon posts a form report with POST (@0x5104) and a Content-Type header (@0x507a). The sandbox shows no DNS and no HTTP for these names, so contact is not observed.
    • dynamic: Two C2 URLs in the extracted config; WinINet resolved; POST and Content-Type strings; sandbox shows no DNS or HTTP to these names.
    • network: The static code is built to beacon over HTTP(S) to the two decoded URLs hxxps://titnovacrion[.]top/live/ and hxxps://skinnyjeanso[.]com/live/. The WinINet calls are resolved by hash, and the code carries the verb "POST" and a form-style report. This is a capability read from the code. No session was observed.
    • network: WinINet resolved by hash; POST and form report strings decoded; candidate URLs decoded from .data. Capability from code; no session observed.
    • network: Report fields for computer name, MAC, OS, user name, adapter config and discovery output (systeminfo, whoami, process list, AV query). Routing is shown; execution and sending are not.
  • T1105 Ingress Tool Transfer:
    • triage: The sample has code paths that fetch a body over WinINet and either write it to disk and run it, or run it from memory. Execution is not shown. - Disk path: codes 0xd, 0xc and 0xf in the dispatcher at 0x3cf4 reach 0x6c2c (code 0xd) and 0x6e04 (codes 0xc and 0xf). Each builds a destination "<shell folder><n>.dll" or ".exe" through the shell-folder routine 0x7e90 and calls 0x6f48 with the URL from its command argument. The disk branch of 0x6f48 calls 0x7acc, which uses 0x77b0 (NtCreateFile) and 0x7a54 (NtWriteFile). 0x6c2c then runs the file through 0xb4cc, which builds "C:\WINDOWS\SYSTEM32\rundll32.exe %s,%s" and calls CreateProcessW; 0x6e04 runs it through 0xb400 (CreateProcessW). - Memory path: 0x7170 (code 0xe) and 0x72c8 (code 0x15) call 0x6f48 with no destination, so the body stays in memory. 0x7170 copies it into a VirtualAlloc region and starts 0xb398 on a thread, which calls into that region. 0x72c8 starts 0xb670, which maps a section and calls through the body pointer. - Caveats: the decompile's comparison of the body against "<html>" and "<!DOCTYPE" (in 0x6f48) uses an output-slot operand, so what exactly it tests is uncertain. The URL and export name arrive as command arguments. The static read does not show their content, so whether the fetch targets the C2 candidates in the IOC claim is not established. - The dispatcher 0x3cf4 is called only by 0x541c, which refers to the decoded COMMAND, URLS and CLEARURL texts.
    • all_tools_qu1cksc0pe: The download routine fetches a payload over HTTP. It reads the reply with InternetReadFile and then either writes the bytes to a file (NtCreateFile, then NtWriteFile) or keeps them in memory when no path is given. This is a file or tool brought onto the host from the network.
    • all_tools_qu1cksc0pe: The sample downloads an EXE, writes it to a "%s%d.exe" path, and starts it with CreateProcessW, so the downloaded program runs as its own process.
    • static: The sample has a code path from a URL fetch to a file write to execution. The fetch routine 0x6f48 calls 0x8d90 (WinINet, resolved by hash) and holds the body in memory. It then calls 0x7acc(path, body, length), which opens the file with NtCreateFile (GENERIC_WRITE, disposition 3) at 0x77b0 and writes it with NtWriteFile at 0x7a54. Two callers pass a path and then execute it. 0x6e04 builds a "%s%d.exe" path (decoded blob 0x10018, referenced at 0x6e55), calls 0x6f48, and calls CreateProcessW at 0xb400 when its flag is set. 0x6c2c builds a "%s%d.dll" path (decoded blob 0xffe0, referenced at 0x6cc5), calls 0x6f48, and launches it through 0xb4cc, which formats "C:\WINDOWS\SYSTEM32\rundll32.exe %s,%s" (blobs 0xfe90 and 0xfef0) and calls CreateProcessW. A third path, 0x7170, calls 0x6f48 with no path. 0x6f48 then returns before any write (param_2 == 0), so the body stays in memory. 0x7170 calls VirtualAlloc, copies the body, and starts a thread at 0xb398. The dispatcher at 0x3cf4 selects these routines by command code (cases 0xc, 0xd, 0xe, 0xf, 0x15). The write branch of 0x6f48 depends on the return sense of 0xbcac, which is not established, so I cannot say which HTML-prefix outcome reaches the write. The sandbox dropped no files (ev_0015) and shows no HTTP session (ev_0026). Whether the command code arrives from the C2 response is not shown. Runtime execution is not observed.
    • static: 0x6f48 fetches a URL via 0x8d90 and writes it through 0x7acc (NtCreateFile/NtWriteFile). Callers 0x6e04 (CreateProcessW at 0xb400) and 0x6c2c (rundll32 via 0xb4cc) execute the written file. 0x7170 runs a fetched body from memory. Runtime execution is not observed; the write branch depends on the unresolved return sense of 0xbcac.
    • static: The sample brings a file onto the host over the network. Routine 0x6f48 fetches a URL through 0x8d90, which calls InternetOpenW and InternetReadFile (ev_0025). If the body does not begin with "<html>" or "<!DOCTYPE" (compare routine 0xbcac, ev_0067), 0x6f48 writes the body to the path it was given, through 0x7acc. 0x7acc calls the NtCreateFile wrapper 0x77b0 (disposition 3, FILE_OPEN_IF) and the NtWriteFile wrapper 0x7a54 (ev_0068, ev_0081). The callers 0x6c2c and 0x6e04 pass a path built from the decoded "%s%d.exe" or "%s%d.dll" under the folder from SHGetFolderPathW(0x1c), which is Local AppData (ev_0069, ev_0021). The file is then started by CreateProcessW in 0xb400 or by the rundll32 command in 0xb4cc (ev_0025). The download is not observed: the sandbox dropped no files (ev_0015).
    • static: The sample has a memory-only variant. Routine 0x7170 calls 0x6f48 with no path (the branch that returns the body without a write, ev_0067). It copies the body into a buffer from VirtualAlloc with PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE (0x40) and starts a thread at 0xb398 with CreateThread. The thread routine calls VirtualFree (ev_0025). 0x72c8 does the same and starts thread 0xb670, which maps a view with CreateFileMappingA and MapViewOfFile (ev_0025). The body is fetched over the network into memory (T1105). The Win32 calls VirtualAlloc and CreateThread start code from that buffer (T1106). Whether the thread executes the buffer as code is not shown.
    • static: 0x6f48 fetches through WinINet, rejects HTML bodies, and writes the rest with 0x7acc (NtCreateFile FILE_OPEN_IF, NtWriteFile). 0x6e04 and 0x6c2c then run the file through CreateProcessW (0xb400 or 0xb4cc). 0x7170 and 0x72c8 run a fetched buffer in an RWX allocation through a thread. No dropped file or child process was observed.
    • static: Beacon 0x5750 sleeps 450–599 s with jitter (1500–2099 s under a flag), posts through WinINet, parses responses in 0x541c, and dispatches to 0x3cf4, which covers discovery, process listing, download-and-run and an update-and-replace path.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Static] Ingress tool transfer over HTTP. Routine 0x6f48 fetches a file. 0x8d90 opens a WinINet session (InternetOpenW with the decoded user agent), opens the URL (InternetOpenUrlW) and reads the body in a loop (InternetReadFile). When a file path is given, 0x6f48 rejects bodies that begin "<html>" or "<!DOCTYPE" and writes the body to disk with 0x7acc (NtCreateFile through 0x77b0, NtWriteFile through 0x7a54, then NtClose). Five callers reach 0x6f48: 0x6c2c, 0x6e04, 0x4110, 0x7170 and 0x72c8. The sandbox recorded no dropped file, so the write is shown in code and not in a run.
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Command dispatch, switch] Routine 0x3cf4 is the command dispatcher. Its only caller is 0x541c (ev_0078), and it receives a numeric id that 0x541c parses (claim 69). Cases: 2 calls 0x1a08 (Desktop listing, "&desklinks=[", with SHGetFolderPathA through the wrapper 0x7f1c); 3 calls 0x1c38 (process list, claim 16, process discovery T1057); 4 starts the discovery thread (claim 31); 0xc calls 0x6e04 and 0xd calls 0x6c2c, both with launch (0xc runs the downloaded EXE directly; 0xd runs the downloaded DLL through rundll32, T1218.011); 0xe calls 0x7170 and 0x15 calls 0x72c8, both of which run downloaded code in memory. The four download-and-run cases 0xc, 0xd, 0xe and 0x15 are ingress tool transfer (T1105; claims 19 and 50). 0xf is self-update (claim 67). 0x11 exits; 0x12 calls 0x4110 (the bp.dat stage); 0x13 and 0x14 set the long-sleep and counter-reset flags. Before the switch, ids 0x12, 0xe, 0xc, 0xd, 0xf, 4 and 0x15 have their argument rewritten (claim 68).
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [In-process download and run] Routine 0x7170 (case 0xe) and 0x72c8 (case 0x15) download a file from the network through 0x6f48 in memory mode (no path, so the body is returned to the caller; claim 19). Case 0xe copies the bytes into a buffer from VirtualAlloc with protection 0x40 (PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE) and runs it on thread 0xb398, which calls the buffer's first word. Case 0x15 starts thread 0xb670 with the buffer, which calls it. This is ingress tool transfer (T1105): a file is brought onto the host over the network and then run. The code then runs inside the sample's own process (claim 51).
    • all_tools_reverser_ghidra: [Command dispatch, switch] Routine 0x3cf4 is the dispatcher. Its cases: 2 calls 0x1a08 (Desktop listing, "&desklinks=[", with SHGetFolderPathA for the desktop); 3 calls 0x1c38 (process list); 4 calls 0x2118 (CreateThread on 0x2200 discovery); 0xc calls 0x6e04 with launch (download an EXE and run it); 0xd calls 0x6c2c with launch (download a DLL and run it through rundll32); 0xe calls 0x7170 (download into memory, VirtualAlloc RWX, run on a new thread); 0xf is a self-update (0x6e04 without launch, close handles 0x3c2c, self-delete 0x3a24, 0x3808, then 0x6e04 with launch, then ExitProcess); 0x11 calls ExitProcess; 0x12 calls 0x4110 (bp.dat stage); 0x13 sets the long-sleep flag DAT 0x1058c; 0x14 sets the counter reset flag DAT 0x10590; 0x15 calls 0x72c8 (download into memory and run). Before the switch, for ids 0x12, 0xe, 0xc, 0xd, 0xf, 4 and 0x15, the argument is matched against the decoded "front" (0xfaf0) and "/files/" (0xfb00) strings and rewritten. That rewrite is unresolved in detail. The dispatcher's only caller is 0x541c.
    • dynamic: The sample brings a file onto the host over the network and then runs it. Routine 0x8d90 opens a WinINet session and reads the response body with InternetReadFile. Routine 0x6f48 wraps it, and callers 0x6c2c and 0x6e04 build the file names "%s%d.dll" and "%s%d.exe". Dispatcher cases 0xc and 0xd (reached from the C2 reply parsed by 0x541c) launch the fetched EXE directly or the DLL through rundll32. Cases 0xe and 0x15 fetch into VirtualAlloc memory and run it on a new thread (0xb398 and 0xb670). The write of the fetched body to disk through 0x7acc is an upstream reading of 0x6f48, not re-read here. The sandbox dropped no file, so the transfer is not observed.
    • dynamic: In-process download and execution path. Routine 0x6f48 reads a URL over WinINet (InternetOpenUrlW and InternetReadFile in 0x8d90) and builds file names "%s%d.dll" and "%s%d.exe" for its callers. Routine 0x7170 calls VirtualAlloc and CreateThread (starting 0xb398); 0x72c8 starts 0xb670, which calls CreateFileMappingA and MapViewOfFile. The "/files/" string is present. The sandbox recorded no dropped files, so the download is not observed. The T1105 rule fires on the InternetReadFile and URLDownloadToFileW pair, with zero benign files and no malware profiles measured.
    • dynamic: 0x6f48 reads URLs via WinINet; 0x7170 and 0x72c8 use VirtualAlloc and CreateThread; no dropped files observed.
    • network: The code downloads a file and runs it. Routine 0x8d90 reads the response body with InternetReadFile, and routine 0x6f48 writes that body to disk through 0x7acc (NtCreateFile via 0x77b0, NtWriteFile via 0x7a54, then NtClose). The download-and-run dispatcher cases then launch it: case 0xc runs an EXE through 0x6e04, case 0xd runs a DLL through rundll32 via 0x6c2c, and cases 0xe and 0x15 run the body in memory. A C2 command would trigger these cases. This is a code-level capability. The sandbox dropped no files, no command reply was observed, and the capture's only file GETs are Windows update CAB files.
    • network: The code can fetch files over the network and run them. WinINet reads a URL, and fetched data is built into "%s%d.dll" and "%s%d.exe" names. None of this is observed: there were no dropped files, and no GET for a sample-named file is in the capture.
    • network: WinINet read path and file-name templates present; URLDownloadToFile resolved; no dropped files; no sample-named file GET in the capture.

A source marked (rule match) or pipeline: is a rule that fired over the binary — capa's over its code, the knowledge table's over its imports — with no sandbox and no model. The rule names are in the Procedure cell, each with its base rate: how much ordinary software it fires on, over the corpus it names. It says nothing about how likely this sample is to be benign, and not measured means the rule has no measurement, not that it never fires.
