10.3 Hunting notes · Written by the report model

  • DNS query logs (Sysmon EventID 22, or resolver logs) and proxy logs for the two FQDNs, and Sysmon EventID 3 with the destination hostname from the TLS SNI or HTTP Host header for titnovacrion[.]top and skinnyjeanso[.]com. (T1071.001)
  • Security event 4698 (scheduled task created) and TaskScheduler/Operational event 106 with the task name Updater, plus a Sysmon EventID 1 for schtasks.exe or taskeng/taskhostw activity whose task XML contains a LogonTrigger and a path under AppData. (T1053.005)
  • Sysmon EventID 1 with Image ending rundll32.exe and CommandLine containing AppData and a comma followed by an export name; the Sigma rule DLL Call by Ordinal Via Rundll32 can be used as a base but needs a path filter. (T1218.011)
  • Sysmon EventID 1 for cmd.exe with a /c argument matching the listed strings, and for wmic.exe with the AntiVirusProduct path; filter on ParentImage rundll32.exe and on child processes spawned with stdout pipes. (T1059.003)
  • Sysmon EventID 15 (FileCreateStreamHash) or a file-system audit for a stream named :wtfbbq, and Sysmon EventID 23 or minifilter telemetry for a delete-on-close request on a process image in AppData. (T1070.004)
  • Proxy or EDR HTTP telemetry for the fixed user-agent string and a POST with Content-Type application/x-www-form-urlencoded; Sysmon EventID 10 or ETW for VirtualAlloc with protection 0x40 followed by CreateThread whose start address falls in that allocation. (T1106)
  • AppLocker or WDAC audit events (AppLocker EventID 8004 or 8025, WDAC CodeIntegrity EventID 3076) for unsigned DLL or EXE loads from %APPDATA% paths, and Sysmon EventID 7 for image loads of unsigned DLLs from those paths. (T1105)

11. Recommendations · Written by the report model

Priority Category Action Rationale Technique
P1 firewall Block or sinkhole titnovacrion[.]top and skinnyjeanso[.]com at the DNS resolver and the web proxy, including the /live/ path prefix, and deny direct outbound HTTP and HTTPS from hosts that are not approved to reach them. The decoded beacon URLs are the only configured C2 endpoints, and blocking them stops the beacon and any fetch-and-run command from reaching those hosts even though no contact was observed in this run. T1071.001
P1 edr_hunting Hunt for a scheduled task named Updater whose exec action points at a per-user Custom_update folder or runs rundll32 against a DLL in that folder, and remove any match after confirming it was not created by an approved administrator. The code builds a logon-triggered task under this name that re-runs the sample's copy, so a matching task is the strongest persistence indicator the code produces. T1053.005
P1 gpo Restrict execution of unsigned DLLs and executables from per-user AppData folders with AppLocker or WDAC rules, except for approved paths, and apply the rule to hosts where the sample has been seen. The sample copies itself into a per-user Custom_update folder and runs a fetched DLL or EXE from AppData, so an execution restriction on that path breaks both the install and the fetch-and-run steps. T1105
P2 edr_hunting Alert on rundll32.exe loading a DLL from a per-user AppData path with a numeric export argument, and on rundll32 command lines of the form path,export where the DLL name has the hash-based pattern of the sample. The code launches its fetched DLL through rundll32 with the path,export format, and the sandbox showed the same launch shape, so the pattern is a direct match for the second-stage launch the code describes. T1218.011
P2 edr_hunting Hunt for cmd.exe or wmic.exe processes whose command lines match the decoded discovery strings (systeminfo, ipconfig /all, whoami /groups, net view /all /domain, nltest /domain_trusts, net group Domain Admins, net config workstation, and the AntiVirusProduct WMI query), especially when the parent is rundll32.exe or an unsigned DLL-hosting process. The decoded strings show that the sample routes these discovery commands through its pipe routine, so their appearance from a process loaded from the sample would indicate its discovery stage running. T1059.003
P2 edr_hunting Hunt for the NTFS alternate data stream name :wtfbbq on any file and for delete-on-close set on a running image, which together mark the sample's self-deletion step. The code renames the running image to the stream :wtfbbq and then sets delete-on-close, so the stream name and the disposition change are specific to this sample. T1070.004
P2 edr_hunting Hunt for a WinINet POST from a DLL-hosting process that sends a form-urlencoded body with the user agent Mozilla/4.0 (compatible; MSIE 7.0; Windows NT 5.1; Tob 1.1), and for a memory allocation with PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE followed by a thread start on that region. The beacon uses this fixed user agent and form report, and the in-memory payload path allocates RWX memory and starts a thread on it, so these two patterns are specific to the sample's beacon and in-memory download code. T1106

ATT&CK mitigations · Reference

ATT&CK's own mitigations (ATT&CK 20.0) for each technique this report publishes; the evidence is what the technique rests on in this run.

Technique Mitigation How it applies Evidence
T1105 Ingress Tool Transfer M1031 Network Intrusion Prevention Network intrusion detection and prevention systems that use network signatures to identify traffic for specific adversary malware or unusual data transfer over known protocols like FTP can be used to mitigate activity at the network level. Signatures are often for unique indicators within protocols and may be based on the specific obfuscation technique used by a particular adversary or tool, and will likely be different across various malware families and versions. Adversaries will likely change tool C2 signatures over time or construct protocols in such a way as to avoid detection by common defensive tools. ev_0015, ev_0019, ev_0020, ev_0021, ev_0025, ev_0026, ev_0050, ev_0067, ev_0068, ev_0069, ev_0075, ev_0077, ev_0078, ev_0081, ev_0082, ev_0086, ev_0092, ev_0107, ev_0112, ev_0116, ev_0129, ev_0132, ev_0145

Nothing recorded in this run: no similarity search returned a match.

12.3 Background · Written by the report model

The sample is a 60,928-byte Windows PE file (MIME application/x-msdownload) [ev_0001]. It was submitted under the file name 6091f2589fef42e0ab3d7975806cd8a0da012b519637c03b73f702f7586b21ef.exe [ev_0047]. Its hashes are SHA-256 6091f2589fef42e0ab3d7975806cd8a0da012b519637c03b73f702f7586b21ef, MD5 71d29d71641017e51b3685ce2f417796, SHA-1 e456eb06758c7349a3636c15c91e1a9c30bb7927 and imphash db7aeb75528663639689f852fd366243 [ev_0002]. The PE header declares a DLL (machine 34404, 0x8664) with five sections, linker 14.0 and four exports, so the .exe suffix of the submitted name does not describe the file type [ev_0004]. The file carries no Authenticode signature [ev_0003]. The evidence does not record where the file was obtained, which source or channel delivered it, or who submitted it; this report does not state those facts.

The analysis combined a triage pack, static parsing of the PE structure, string and capability scans, emulated decoding of the stored strings, a function-level read of the code, a sandbox run and a packet capture of 1,474 packets over 576.0 s [ev_0005] [ev_0008] [ev_0019] [ev_0021] [ev_0017]. In the sandbox the file was launched as a DLL through rundll32 with the export ordinal #1; that launch is the harness's action, and the sandbox recorded three processes in total [ev_0012] [ev_0028]. The sandbox report lacks its API-call, registry, file, mutex, service and event sections, and it dropped no files [ev_0026] [ev_0015]. The sandbox network section lists two hosts [ev_0013].

For reputation, VirusTotal flags the file as malicious in 56 of 75 engines [ev_0018]. The local YARA corpus gives 0 hits of 31 rules [ev_0007]. The platform's classification reads downloader / backdoor with family Latrodectus. Engine labels disagree on family and several name IcedID, and the sandbox signature names a Latrodectus loader [ev_0014] [ev_0018]. This report treats the family as not established by the label alone.

Values in Measured sections come from tools and are reported as the tools returned them. Assessed sections are the models' conclusions, reported as they wrote them; the checks above are the platform's comments on them.

13.1 Claims whose code locations or API names the body does not name · Measured

A claim in force is listed here when the body does not name it by its label and does not name one or more of its code locations (function names of four hex digits or more) or API-style names; each row lists the names the body lacks. A claim that names none of these is listed when the body carries half or fewer of its words of five letters or more.

  • all_tools_qu1cksc0pe: 3 claims carried forward unwritten by a revision (from round 1) are not discussed in the body; each is listed in the evidence bundle.
  • all_tools_static_r2: 2 claims carried forward unwritten by a revision (from round 2) are not discussed in the body; each is listed in the evidence bundle.
  • static: 1 claim carried forward unwritten by a revision (from round 1) is not discussed in the body; each is listed in the evidence bundle.
  • triage claim 8 (technique T1053.005; confidence 0.75; the body never names NewTask): The sample registers a logon-triggered scheduled task named "Updater", with an exec action that runs the sample's own module path with arguments. The read chain is: export extra at 0x3ce4, then 0x3cb4, then 0x3868, then 0x33ac (taken only when the gate at 0x378c returns 0), then 0x3728, then 0xb344, then COM creation at 0xc5e8 (CoCreateInstance, per the resolved names), then 0xc42c. In 0xc42c, the NewTask call on the service object is followed by 0xc2c4, which creates a trigger of type 9 (logon) with the Id "LogonTrigger" (decoded at 0x10030). The user name comes from GetUserNameA (0x8ae0). Then 0xc19c creates the exec action, with the path from 0x638 and the arguments from 0x428. Finally the folder object is called at vtable +0x88 with flags 6 (create or update). The name "Updater" is decoded at 0xf988 and formatted in 0x33ac. Three limits apply. The vtable offsets are read from the decompile, and the identification as RegisterTaskDefinition is an inference from the argument pattern. The gate 0x378c is known only by its callees (0x3250, 0x6b9c and 0x77b0, an NtCreateFile open); its body is not in the ledger, so reading it as a presence check is inference. 0x33ac also copies a file with 0x7504, from the module path that 0x463c obtains through GetModuleFileNameW; the destination's construction (0x3250, 0x30d0) is not decoded here. Registration in any run is not established. - Run-key and Startup-folder persistence is not asserted. The function rows read show registry calls only as NtOpenKey (0xb0c4) and NtQueryValueKey (0xb1d4, with NtClose); no Run-key or Startup-folder write appears in them. It tests the path with 0x8fe8, which copies the first four characters with 0xbb2c and compares them with the literal "??" at 0x10200 through 0xb900. If the prefix is missing, it builds the prefix from the literal at 0x10210 (also "??") followed by the path, using 0xba98; it frees the old path, replaces it and returns 1. If the prefix is present it returns 1 unchanged. On failure it frees the partial result and returns 0. Callers: 0x6f48 (before the write), 0x33ac, 0x378c, 0x3808, 0x4110 and 0xd1c8. 0xba98 is named but its body is not read; its append role is taken from its call sites. Evidence: ev_0068 (0x6b9c body); ev_0096 (bytes at 0x10210 are "??"); ev_0099 (bytes at 0x10200 are "??"); ev_0098 (0x8fe8 body; 0x3808 and 0xd1c8 call 0x6b9c); ev_0067 (0x6f48 calls 0x6b9c); ev_0086 (0x33ac calls 0x6b9c); ev_0094 (0x378c calls 0x6b9c); ev_0077 (0x4110 calls 0x6b9c).
  • all_tools_reverser_ghidra claim 61 (confidence 0.85; the body never names FUN_1360bc0bb2c): [Static] Routine 0xbb2c (FUN_1360bc0bb2c) duplicates a wide string of n characters. It allocates 2n+2 zeroed bytes with 0xad34 and copies 2n bytes from the source. It returns the new block, or 0 if the allocation fails. Callers: 0x6f48 (duplicates the URL), 0x463c, 0x4110, 0x33ac, and 0x8fe8 (the four-character prefix test in claim 61). Evidence: ev_0068 (0xbb2c body); ev_0067 (0x6f48 call); ev_0069 (0x463c call); ev_0077 (0x4110 call); ev_0086 (0x33ac call); ev_0098 (0x8fe8 call).
  • all_tools_reverser_ghidra claim 63 (confidence 0.85; the body never names FUN_1360bc01868): [Static] Routine 0x1868 (FUN_1360bc01868) frees the result table of the discovery thread. The table is 0x58 bytes, holding 11 output buffers (one per command that 0x2200 routes to 0x1030) and the table itself. It frees each buffer and the table, then nulls the pointer. 0x2200 calls it after it concatenates the outputs into the cached buffer DAT 0x10548 (claim 15), so the cached text survives while the per-command buffers are freed. Evidence: ev_0098 (0x1868 body); ev_0067 (0x2200 calls 0x1868 on its 11-slot table).
  • all_tools_reverser_ghidra claim 66 (technique T1070.004; confidence 0.80; the body never names RtlInitUnicodeString): [Static] Routine 0x3808 deletes the installed copy. It builds the install path with 0x30d0 (AppData through 0x7b98(0), the folder "Custom_update" and the volume-derived "Update_%x" name, claim 72) and 0x3250 (the .dll or .exe extension). It normalises the path (0x6b9c) and deletes it through the NtDeleteFile wrapper 0x7694 (index slots NtDeleteFile and RtlInitUnicodeString). It returns 1 on success. Its caller is the self-update case 0xf of 0x3cf4, after the self-rename (0x3a24) and before the relaunch. The sequence is: download the new copy (0x6e04 without launch), close handles (0x3c2c), rename the running image to the stream ":wtfbbq" and set delete-on-close (0x3a24, claim 43), delete the old install copy (0x3808), and start the new copy (0x6e04 with launch). The argument 0x3808 receives is passed to 0x30d0 and 0x3250, which ignore it, so the deleted path is the install path whatever the argument. This is deletion of the sample's own copy (T1070.004). Evidence: ev_0098 (0x3808 body); ev_0077 (0x3cf4 case 0xf body; 0x30d0 body); ev_0086 (0x3250 and 0x3194 bodies); ev_0025 (0x7694 slot names).
  • all_tools_reverser_ghidra claim 69 (technique T1082; confidence 0.70; the body never names FindVolumeClose): [Static] Routine 0x7b80 derives the host key DAT 0x10458 from disk volume information. It calls 0x89d4 (volume enumeration; its index row lists FindFirstVolumeW, GetVolumeInformationW and FindVolumeClose) and passes the result to 0x8bbc (body not read). The routine is declared void, and its caller 0x463c stores whatever 0x8bbc leaves in RAX. The value is the RC4 key for the configuration file (claim 65) and the guid field of the beacon report. Reading volume information is a read of the host's disk configuration (T1082). Evidence: ev_0098 (0x7b80 body); ev_0069 (0x463c stores the 0x7b80 result as DAT 0x10458); ev_0067 (0x5750 passes DAT 0x10458 into the report); ev_0025 (0x89d4 slot names).
  • all_tools_reverser_ghidra claim 73 (confidence 0.85; the body never names FUN_1360bc06864): [Static] Routine 0x6864 (FUN_1360bc06864) decodes the base64 alphabet at 0x10160 ("ABC…+/") into a heap copy and stores the pointer in DAT 0x10540. It returns nonzero on success. The encoder 0x6634 uses that pointer, and the decoder 0x6370 returns nothing unless DAT 0x10540 is set, so the reply decoder depends on this call. 0x3868 treats a failure as a gate (claim 39). Evidence: ev_0100 (0x6864 body); ev_0082 (0x6370 body returns null when DAT 0x10540 is 0); ev_0090 (0x6634 body); ev_0021 (alphabet at 0x10160, referred at 0x6873 in 0x6864); ev_0069 (0x3868 calls 0x6864).
  • dynamic claim 2 (confidence 0.85; the body never names LOLBin): The sandbox launched the sample's export itself. The command line rundll32.exe ...6091f2589f...dll,#1 appears for pid 83 (parent 56) and pid 88 (parent 82). The Sigma rule "DLL Call by Ordinal Via Rundll32" and the rundll32 LOLBin hit fire on these harness lines. This is the harness's launch, not behaviour of the sample, and the parents 56 and 82 are not in the report. Evidence: ev_0102, ev_0009, ev_0011, ev_0028
  • dynamic claim 31 (confidence 0.80; the body never names RedLineStealer, DarkComet, LummaStealer, RaccoonStealer): The family is attributed to Latrodectus by the sandbox and by the extracted configuration, but the static features do not confirm it. The sandbox signatures name a Latrodectus loader at severity 10, and the extracted configuration reports family "latrodectus". The VirusTotal labels also name IcedID. The fingerprint lookup returns RedLineStealer (0.7508), Pony (0.7385), DarkComet (0.7355), LummaStealer (0.7349) and RaccoonStealer (0.729), which lie within 0.022 of one another, so it does not discriminate. Evidence: ev_0104, ev_0106, ev_0018, ev_0109, ev_0108, ev_0020
  • dynamic claim 40 (technique T1105; confidence 0.55; the body never names URLDownloadToFileW): In-process download and execution path. Routine 0x6f48 reads a URL over WinINet (InternetOpenUrlW and InternetReadFile in 0x8d90) and builds file names "%s%d.dll" and "%s%d.exe" for its callers. Routine 0x7170 calls VirtualAlloc and CreateThread (starting 0xb398); 0x72c8 starts 0xb670, which calls CreateFileMappingA and MapViewOfFile. The "/files/" string is present. The sandbox recorded no dropped files, so the download is not observed. The T1105 rule fires on the InternetReadFile and URLDownloadToFileW pair, with zero benign files and no malware profiles measured. Evidence: [ev_0021] "%s%d.dll"@0x6cc5, "%s%d.exe"@0x6e55, "/files/"@0xfb00; [ev_0019] "/files/"@0x3efa, "files/bp.dat"@0x431b; [ev_0025] 0x8d90, 0x7170, 0x72c8, 0xb398, 0xb670; [ev_0020] VirtualAlloc @0x948f; [ev_0107] T1105 rule; [ev_0112] 0 dropped
  • dynamic claim 43 (confidence 0.80; the body never names RedLineStealer, DarkComet, LummaStealer, RaccoonStealer): Family attribution: Latrodectus. The sandbox signature "Detect larodectus Loader variant 2" and "Family: Latrodectus loader" (severity 10) and the extracted configuration family "latrodectus" agree. The VT labels mix Latrodectus with IcedID, and some name coinminers and keyloggers, which no decoded string or resolved API corroborates (no keylogging hook API is in the resolved set). The fingerprint lookup gave RedLineStealer 0.7508, Pony 0.7385, DarkComet 0.7355, LummaStealer 0.7349 and RaccoonStealer 0.729. None is Latrodectus and all five sit within 0.022 of one another, so the lookup does not discriminate. The similar-case lookup gives generic T1027 and T1129 with no category. Evidence: [ev_0104] signatures; [ev_0106] family latrodectus; [ev_0018] VT labels; [ev_0109] family lookup; [ev_0108] similar cases; [ev_0020] resolved set
  • network claim 2 (confidence 0.70; the body never names oBFYYahzgVI): The sandbox network section lists two hosts, UDP to 8[.]8[.]8[.]8:53 and TCP to 142[.]251[.]30[.]94:80. Neither is attributed to a process of the sample. The TCP host carries the domain tag c[.]pki[.]goog. The capture's HTTP to c[.]pki[.]goog requests /r/r1.crl and /wr2/oBFYYahzgVI.crl, which are certificate revocation list paths. This reads as an operating-system trust check and not as C2. Isolation: where this run read the sample's bytes, and what kept each reader from the worker, as measured when each started; never assumed.

  • Not every source that read the sample's bytes was kept from the worker; each is listed with what was measured.
  • The worker process runs as user 1000; its environment holds the platform's own variables DATABASE_URL, JWT_SECRET_KEY, MINIO_ACCESS_KEY, MINIO_ROOT_PASSWORD, MINIO_ROOT_USER, MINIO_SECRET_KEY, POSTGRES_PASSWORD, REDIS_PASSWORD, REDIS_URL, SETTINGS_ENCRYPTION_KEY.
  • Triage pack (pack) read the sample in the worker process itself: not isolated, since it shares the worker's memory, environment, files and network.
  • The report step (report) read the sample in the worker process itself: not isolated, since it shares the worker's memory, environment, files and network.
  • The platform's own readers in the worker (worker) read the sample in the worker process itself: not isolated, since it shares the worker's memory, environment, files and network.
  • Tool server qu1cksc0pe (stdio, run on this machine) read the sample in a process the worker started; the run holds no measurement of the process it was started as, so its isolation cannot be told.
  • The r2 provider's tool server (stdio, run on this machine) read the sample. The process the worker started for it was measured at its start: it ran as user 1000 (one of its real, effective, saved or filesystem user ids is the worker's); in a group of the worker's; sharing the worker's process table; the worker's mount namespace; the worker's network; no effective, permitted or ambient capabilities; no bar on gaining privileges; its environment held none of the platform's own variables. It had no descendant process when it was measured. So: not isolated.
  • The ghidra provider's tool server (http) at localhost read the sample; it is a server reached at a local address, which this run does not measure, so not verified by this run.
  • The sample the platform handed out (3 copies) was hashed when the job started and when the run ended, and every reading was its intake SHA-256 6091f2589fef42e0ab3d7975806cd8a0da012b519637c03b73f702f7586b21ef.

Appendix D. Methodology · Reference

This report was produced by an automated team of language-model analysts working over tool servers. The analysts' claims were negotiated, a judge stated the verdict and the STIX bundle, and a report model wrote the assessed prose. Every tool call is in Appendix A. No table value in the body of the report is cut. A table value in Appendix A that ends in … is cut to fit the page; the JSON report and the evidence ledger (Appendix A's ids) carry it whole. Code is never cut to fit the page: Appendix A draws each function's code whole, as a listing.

  • Team: all_tools (triage, static, all_tools_static_r2, all_tools_qu1cksc0pe, all_tools_reverser_ghidra, dynamic, network)
  • Stages run: triage_pack, triage, static, reversing, dynamic, network, debate, verdict, report
  • Tool servers called: analysis, knowledge, network, pipeline, qu1cksc0pe, virustotal
  • Sandbox: recorded the sample's behaviour
  • Report generated: 2026-11-06T16:02:33.729720+00:00
